## 2026-10-23 01:58 UTC incident: train 13 red on INT (lead)
- CI #1439 on INT 604f9560: Backend app tests failed 1 of 420 (DataFlowTraceTest hop 11, qa e6650974): the test expected a routes_not_sales_submitted warning for the unplanned route GUL-SR-1; backend-core 24b08bd6 (D-BC-PREVIEW-01, lead ruling) counts warnings only for due routes, so the preview returns none. Both heads were green on their own runs against bases that lacked the other: a semantic interaction between two file-disjoint heads, not a merge conflict and not a flake; no re-run. The deploy was skipped, dev stays on 52bdd477 (train 12); V0109 is on INT but not deployed.
- Fix path: qa #2 changes the test to the ruled semantics (no warning for a not-due route, a due route without a Sales Submit still warns), on a head merging 604f9560; one push; its lane run green → lead check → integrator 17 pushes it as train 13.1. Everything else holds. Honest times: 13.1 push about 02:50, dev about 04:00 UTC.
- Lesson for the gate: a head accepted on the disjoint-base clause (backend-core) can still interact semantically with a sibling head's tests; INT CI is the check for that, and it worked before any deploy. Keep the clause, but when a ruling changes behaviour that another lane's test encodes, tell that lane in the same message as the ruling.

Real phones take server-issued numbers: unaffected. Second finding: the deploy workflow reports success with a failed smoke table (no gate).

Lesson: a smoke that never reaches its real step is not a passing smoke; the deploy must fail on a FAILED smoke row.

- P0: android-print #4 (the archived-in-spirit 508k session, still alive) found the real root cause of every print failure: PrintTemplate.TOKEN = `\{(@?)([^{}]{1,60})}` ends in a bare `}`; Android's ICU regex rejects it (U_REGEX_RULE_SYNTAX) while the desktop JVM accepts it, so every memo/stock-slip/summary template failed to parse on the phone and no test saw it. Fix 72ff0a37 (braces escaped; AndroidRegexBracesTest applies ICU's rule to every regex literal in android/ and shared/, fails on the old pattern; guardedPrint logs to CrashLog); Opus T1 confirmed with the ICU 74 library; lane run 40096957341 (#1542) running. Lead git check: based on ec76aa5f; delta core-printing PrintTemplate.kt + test, feature-sale PrintGuard.kt (6 lines, android-tso's file, accepted for the P0; android-tso #6 told on creation), docs/status/android-print.md; no DECISIONS bullet yet (asked for one in house style); no overlap with the train 30 heads. ### 14:15 UTC 2026-11-05 — ROOT CAUSE of deploys #308/#309/#311 found by db #15 and reproduced: idle_in_transaction_session_timeout kills Flyway's second connection
- db #15 (14:13): Flyway on PostgreSQL uses TWO connections — the migration runs on one; the other (schema-history/lock connection) sits IDLE IN A TRANSACTION for the whole migration. The dev server has `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 30000` (postgres.bicep:126, confirmed by ops-diagnose), so the server terminates that session 30 s into any migration; Flyway notices only on its next write (the history insert, ~80 ms after the body ends) → SQLSTATE 08006, "Unable to rollback transaction", "Unable to restore connection to its original state". Reproduced with the project's own Flyway on PG16 (throwaway, not committed): `SELECT pg_sleep(8); CREATE TABLE t_idle` under a 3 s timeout → FlywaySqlException, table not created; timeout 0 → OK; the dev value 30 s with pg_sleep(35) → the same exception. So ANY migration longer than ~30 s fails on dev whatever its SQL: V0144 v2 (0.9 s) and V0145 (0.3 s) passed; the shipped V0144 body (105 s) and V0146 (104 s) completed and then failed. Platform, client, OOM, network and lock contention are all excluded; the lead's 08006 reading ("server closed within 80 ms of the last answer") was right about the where and wrong about the why — the close had happened at second 30 on the OTHER connection. The tcpKeepAlive line of train 77 is harmless but not the remedy (recorded).
- Lead decisions (14:16): V0146/V0147 stay exactly as shipped (no in-place replacement; the batched design becomes a fleet-size note in db #15's fleet plan). Fix = lead hotfix train 78 on lane/lead-migrate-idle from INT 77 (98d1c219): Flyway `initSql("SET idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 0")` in the Migrator (scope: the migrate role's connections only; api/worker pools keep the 30 s protection), a regression test running the real Migrator against a database with the timeout at 2 s and a 5 s migration, DECISIONS D-OPS-MIGRATE-IDLE-TIMEOUT-01, and `infra/migrations.hold` DELETED so the deploy runs V0146–V0148 for real with the fixed image (the migrate job runs the image built from the same commit). db #15 asked to confirm the fix independently in its harness once the branch is pushed. Integrator standing by.
- 14:19: ops-diagnose runs 42042818353 (13:50–14:05) and 42042825102 (09:40–09:56) with train 77's PostgreSQLLogs query (diagnostic setting "to-log-analytics", categoryGroup allLogs enabled → the server log IS in Log Analytics). SERVER LOG CONFIRMS db #15's root cause for #309: `09:45:57 UTC-6aec502c.f595f-LOG: disconnection: session time: 0:00:40.388 user=aronadmin database=aron host=10.51.0.128` + same session `FATAL: terminating connection due to idle-in-transaction timeout` = Flyway's second (schema-history/lock) session, opened 09:45:17, killed 30 s after V0144 began at 09:45:27; the migration session disconnected 09:47:12 after 0:01:46 (the 105 s body). Also visible: app_jobs (worker) session of 28 min disconnecting at 09:45:28, the 02:00 azuresu maintenance sessions, checkpoints. Nothing else: no crash, no OOM, no restart.
- Train 78 written on lane/lead-migrate-idle (from INT 77 98d1c219): backend/platform Database.kt `Migrator.INIT_SQL = "SET idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = 0"` applied through Flyway `initSql` in a new `Migrator.configure(ds, connectRetries, interval, locations)` (migrate() unchanged in behaviour otherwise); `MigratorIdleTimeoutTest` (3 tests: 5 s migration under a 2 s per-session timeout set by the pgjdbc `options` connection parameter passes with the exemption and fails without it; the exemption is in the default configuration); DECISIONS +1 (D-OPS-MIGRATE-IDLE-TIMEOUT-01, credit db #15) = 247; `infra/migrations.hold` deleted (the deploy runs V0146–V0148 for real with the fixed migrate image). A throwaway local PostgreSQL 16 (initdb as user pgtest, trust auth, db aron_test) runs the new test through Gradle before the push.
- 14:20: #311 confirmed the same way (run 42042818353, window 13:50–14:05): `13:55:26 UTC-6aec8aae.fce90-FATAL: terminating connection due to idle-in-transaction timeout` + `disconnection: session time: 0:00:32.609 user=aronadmin … host=10.51.0.128` (Flyway's second session, killed 30 s after V0146 began at 13:54:56); the migration session `6aec8aae.fce91` disconnected 13:56:41 after 0:01:46.753 (the 104 s body); a third aronadmin session of 0.37 s at 13:56:41 (Flyway's cleanup). Two incidents, one cause, both now in the record.

### 17:03 UTC 2026-11-05 — dblogins failure cause: train 77's tcpKeepAlive breaks psql (lead regression); hotfix train 79
- ops-diagnose run 42070194557 (16:38–16:58, job_prefix caj-aron-dev-dblogins): the execution DID get a replica (SuccessfulCreate / AssigningReplica caj-aron-dev-dblogins-lgzby2d-276wl 16:44:03; image pulled) and its only console line, 16:44:26, is `psql: error: invalid URI query parameter: "tcpKeepAlive"`. The dblogins job (and the seed execution on the same job) run `exec psql "$ARON_DB_URL#jdbc:" …`: the direct JDBC URL with "jdbc:" stripped is a libpq URI, and the `tcpKeepAlive=true` that train 77 added to directParams (D-INFRA-DIRECT-URL-KEEPALIVE-01) is a pgjdbc-only parameter → psql exits at once; "No replicas found for execution" was the already-exited replica; the 12-minute "no console log" was ingestion lag. A lead regression (nobody checked the non-JDBC consumers of the direct URL; the checker had even noted db-login-secrets.sh inherits the pairs). The keepalive was not the cause of the 08006 incident either (D-OPS-MIGRATE-IDLE-TIMEOUT-01).
- Also in the window: worker revisions --0000143 (paused, 16:40:16) and --0000144 (resumed, 16:43:54) created by the new pause mechanism — it works at the platform level; the migrate job's two aronadmin sessions disconnected at 16:43:39 after 1:53 and 1:49 (the migrations ran ≈110 s and recorded cleanly: no FATAL, no idle-in-transaction line in the window → the exemption works on dev). Server log also shows app_jobs "permission denied for table bundle_snapshot" (16:41:56–16:45:32) and "outlet_request_event" (16:51:24): the worker (train 72 image, app_jobs role) after the migrations and WITHOUT the runtime-logins.sql grants run (dblogins failed) — to be re-checked after the next successful deploy; if they persist they are a pre-existing grant gap for db #15.
- Deploy attempt 2 (started 17:00) will fail the same way at dblogins after a no-op migrate; left to finish (harmless; confirms). Hotfix train 79 = lane/lead-urlfix from INT 78 (8f83a122): remove `tcpKeepAlive=true` from keyvault.bicep directParams (back to sslmode/user/password), a check_infra test asserting the direct URL's query keys are libpq-recognised (the URL has two consumers: pgjdbc and psql), DECISIONS D-INFRA-DIRECT-URL-KEEPALIVE-02 (reverted; the lesson). - 2026-11-06 02:49: overnight scheduled runs on INT: CodeQL 42144566891 success (01:32); **acr-purge 42144981907 (#5) FAILED** (01:36, job 127654157888): `infra/scripts/acr-purge.sh rg-aron-dev 30` listed the images in use (backend c841d459…, web afe4bf45…, tools/postgres 721873c3…) and then `az acr manifest delete` of aron-backend@sha256:0734cfab… (tag df6b8899…, an old commit) failed with "The operation is disallowed on this registry, repository or image" → "::error::could not delete …". Cause (lead reading, from deploy.sh:415-439): the deploy locks every pushed tag with `az acr repository update --image <repo>:<sha> --write-enabled false --delete-enabled true`; deleting a manifest removes its tags, which ACR treats as a write on a write-locked tag → disallowed. Fix for the next lead ops train (not urgent, housekeeping; pilot-size registry): unlock each tag of a candidate manifest (`--write-enabled true`) before the manifest delete, or delete by tag after unlocking; add a check_infra test pairing the deploy's lock with the purge's unlock; the weekly schedule is `17 21 * * 4`. No action under the stop rule; noted for the morning report.

### 2026-11-11 02:18 UTC — qa-ci root cause of the `stk_save not found` load-only flake (lead record)
- qa-ci #1 (02:14): the Stock host composes StockContent (the only place the `stk_save` Save button exists) only after AronLoaded(rememberAsyncLoad(epoch){ day.stockLoad()… slipNotPrinted(), stockSlipPrintable() }) returns (AmoSaleHosts.kt:262-276 and the SR equivalent); those reads run on Room's executor, which Compose waitForIdle() does not wait for; AmoRefusalHostsTest.kt:82 and SrRefusalHostsTest.kt:81 tap SAVE right after show(). Under load the read is late → "could not find any node … 'stk_save'". Test-side missing await, not a product fault. Reproduced deterministically with a temporary Thread.sleep(1200) in slipNotPrinted() (reverted): both fail unfixed, pass fixed. Fix (test-only): awaitStockLoaded() = awaitSettled + waitForIdle + waitUntil the Save node exists (30 s guard); ×5 cache off green; D-QA-CI-14 also corrects the "Saturday" slip. Lead: push approved; a Haiku-inventoried sweep of the same tap-after-show() shape on async-loaded hosts approved as one further push (D-QA-CI-15), or into HANDOVER if context runs short.

## 2026-11-11 07:04 UTC — A2 #4 flake root cause delivered (d9d0e7178) (lead)
- 07:04 A2 #4 reported verbatim: "checked d9d0e7178 (lane/android-amo-calls; contains the current INT 05a50235b = train 90). Flake root cause found and fixed in the harness; D-AMO-CALLS-32 has the record. Gate: Gradle exit 0 for feature-amo + app-amo tests (118 in app-amo, 0 failures), lintDebug ALL modules, assemble app-amo/sr/tso, screenshot verify, tools/ci test_gates. ROOT CAUSE, read from the full stack in the attempt-1 job log (job 130366917888): the bottom of the stack is DrainableExecutor.execute, a Room executor thread. createEmptyComposeRule() runs the test Recomposer on an UnconfinedTestDispatcher, so a composition coroutine (LaunchedEffect, rememberAsyncLoad) that awaited a Room query is resumed INLINE on the Room thread. The test's ApplyingContinuationInterceptor then calls Snapshot.sendApplyNotifications() on that thread, which wakes the Recomposer there, and when a frame is due the whole frame (every effect that enters composition) runs on test-room-io-N. activity-compose's BackHandler uses LifecycleStartEffect, so it throws. Dispatchers.Main = MainLooperImmediate (D-QA-CI-11) does not cover it, because the Recomposer does not use Dispatchers.Main. REPRODUCED by a probe that models the mechanism (200 Room-thread resumptions, each adding an effect): default rule, 1 of 150 effect runs off-main, twice in a row; StandardTestDispatcher, 0 of 150. HONEST: 20 runs of the class under 4 busy loops were green BEFORE the fix, so the repeated runs prove nothing on their own. After the fix, 20 runs of AmoControlCallHostTest and 3 full app-amo suites under load were green, but the proof is the probe. FIX (test-only, no assertion touched, no @Ignore or retry): hostComposeRule() = createEmptyComposeRule(StandardTestDispatcher()), used by the 5 app-amo classes that compose against a real AmoDay (AmoControlCallHostTest, AmoDayLockHostsTest, AmoLiveDashboardHostTest, AmoRefusalHostsTest, AmoTeamPerformanceHostTest), plus the regression test HostComposeRuleTest. FOR YOU (not changed, outside this lane's files): the app-sr host classes with createEmptyComposeRule() (SrRefusalHostsTest, SrDayLockHostsTest, SrDayLockHomeTest, SrDayLockBannerTest, SrFrameTest; the feature-tickets tests too) have the same exposure. D-QA-CI-16's SR failure at LifecycleRegistry.kt:382 is very likely the same mechanism. The recipe is the one-line swap in D-32 (4). D-31 LOWS (all done): KDoc of RouteTargetsRead, AmoFetch.Gone and loadTargetNodes corrected; the record says the loop ends on ANY zone Gone (403/404/501); the memo guard now guards the first value found; a refused zone drops the kept route copies (test); a zone-sum test with a negative member; the double forget removed. Holding."
- Lead: static gate and a narrow Opus pass (main code touched: memo guard, kept-copy purge, forget) follow; train 91 on PASS. The same-mechanism exposure in app-sr and feature-tickets host tests is assigned to A2 #4 as the next round (test-only recipe, D-QA-CI-17), since no qa-ci lane is active; then A2 #4 is recycled with a HANDOVER successor.
